        1 -# Oscilloscope Front-End Circuit

        1 +# ATmega32A — Function Generator + Dual-Path Oscilloscope     
        2 +## Complete KiCad Circuit Reference                           
        3  
        4 -> **Safety:** This circuit is NOT safe for mains voltages.    
        5 -> There is no galvanic isolation between the probe and the MCU
          -.                                                             
        6 -> Use for bench signals only: DC or AC ≤ 30V.                 
        7 -> Never connect to mains, motor drives, or any live electrical
          - equipment.                                                   
        4 +> **Safety:** No mains connection. Bench/low-voltage signals o
          +nly (≤ 30V).                                                  
        5 +> No galvanic isolation in this design.

       11 -## Overview                                                   
        9 +## System Block Diagram                                       
       10  
       11  ```
       14 -Probe ──[Fuse+MOV]──[R_in 10kΩ]──[Clamp diodes]──[2.5V bias]  
       15 -                                                        │     
       16 -                                              NE5532-B (+) com
          -parator                                                       
       17 -                                                        │     
       18 -16-bit R-2R DAC (2× 74HC595) ──────── NE5532-B (−) reference  
       19 -                                                        │     
       20 -                              NE5532-B output → 6N137 → ATmega
          - PD2                                                          
       21 -                                                              
          -│                                                             
       22 -                                                   SAR firmwar
          -e (16 steps)                                                  
       23 -                                                              
          -│                                                             
       24 -                                                      UART → P
          -C                                                             
       12 +                    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────
          +────────┐                                                     
       13 +                    │                 ATmega32A (U1)          
          +         │                                                    
       14 +                    │                                         
          +          │                                                   
       15 +  PROBE ──[PROTECT]─┤─ PA0 ──────────────── Internal 10-bit AD
          +C        │                                                    
       16 +          [BIAS]    │                        (fast, ~38kSPS)  
          +          │                                                   
       17 +              │     ├─ PD2 ←── 6N137 ←── NE5532-B ←─ BIAS NODE
          +        │                                                     
       18 +              │     │                         ↑               
          +          │                                                   
       19 +              │     │         16-bit R-2R DAC (2× 74HC595)    
          +         │                                                    
       20 +              │     ├─ PB3 (latch) ───────────────────── ↑    
          +         │                                                    
       21 +              │     ├─ PB5 (MOSI) ────────────────────── │    
          +         │                                                    
       22 +              │     ├─ PB7 (SCK) ─────────────────────── │    
          +         │                                                    
       23 +              │     │                                         
          +          │                                                   
       24 +              │     ├─ PC0-PC7 ─── 8-bit R-2R DAC ──► FG OUTPU
          +T       │                                                     
       25 +              │     │                                         
          +          │                                                   
       26 +              │     ├─ PD0/PD1 ─── UART (RX/TX)               
          +        │                                                     
       27 +              │     ├─ PD5/PD6/PD7 ─── LED status             
          +        │                                                     
       28 +              └─────┤─ NE5532-B (+) (comparator input)        
          +         │                                                    
       29 +                    └─────────────────────────────────────────
          +────────┘                                                     
       30  ```
       31  
       27 -The 6N137 provides a digital isolation barrier between the com
          -parator output                                                
       28 -and the MCU. It protects PD2 from spikes during probe faults o
          -r overvoltage.

       34 +## Schematic 1 — Input Protection & Bias                      
       35  
       36 +**Net names:** PROBE_TIP, PROBE_FUSED, PROBE_CLAMPED, BIAS_NOD
          +E                                                             
       37 +                                                              
       38  ```
       35 -PROBE ──[F1 100mA slow-blow]──[MOV S14K275]──[R_in 10kΩ 1W]   
       36 -                                                      │       
       37 -                                         [1N4148]── +5V  ← pos
          -itive clamp                                                   
       38 -                                         [1N4148]── GND  ← neg
          -ative clamp                                                   
       39 -                                                      │       
       40 -                                                  to Stage 2  
       39 +PROBE_TIP                                                     
       40 +    │                                                         
       41 +   [F1] 100mA 250V slow-blow                                  
       42 +    │                                                         
       43 +PROBE_FUSED                                                   
       44 +    │                                                         
       45 +   [MOV1] S14K275  (across PROBE_FUSED to GND)                
       46 +    │                                                         
       47 +   [R1] 10kΩ 1W                                               
       48 +    │                                                         
       49 +PROBE_CLAMPED ──────────────────────────────────────────── to 
          +PA0 (U1 pin 40)                                               
       50 +    │                                                         
       51 +   [D1] 1N4148 anode→PROBE_CLAMPED, cathode→+5V    (positive c
          +lamp)                                                         
       52 +   [D2] 1N4148 anode→GND,           cathode→PROBE_CLAMPED (neg
          +ative clamp)                                                  
       53 +    │                                                         
       54 +   [R2] 10kΩ                                                  
       55 +    │                                                         
       56 +BIAS_NODE ──────────────────────────────────────────────── to 
          +U2 pin 5 (+)                                                  
       57 +    │                                                         
       58 +   [R3] 10kΩ    (R2 + R3 form 2.5V divider from +5V to GND)   
       59 +    │                                                         
       60 +   GND                                                        
       61 +    │                                                         
       62 +   [C1] 100nF    (across R3, i.e. BIAS_NODE to GND — noise fil
          +ter)                                                          
       63 +                                                              
       64 +                                                              
       65 ++5V ──[R2=10kΩ]──BIAS_NODE──[R3=10kΩ]──GND                    
       66 +                     │                                        
       67 +                    [C1] 100nF                                
       68 +                     │                                        
       69 +                    GND                                       
       70  ```

       43 -| Component | Value | Purpose |                               
       44 -|-----------|-------|---------|                               
       45 -| F1 | 100mA 250V slow-blow | overcurrent protection |        
       46 -| MOV1 | S14K275 (or V275LA10A) | transient suppression |     
       47 -| R_in | 10kΩ 1W | fault current limiter |                    
       48 -| D1 | 1N4148 to +5V | positive overvoltage clamp |           
       49 -| D2 | 1N4148 to GND | negative overvoltage clamp |           
       72 +**Component values:**                                         
       73  
       51 -After R_in + clamps, the signal node stays within −0.7V to +5.
          -7V regardless                                                 
       52 -of probe fault. No current flows backward into the MCU even du
          -ring a spike.                                                 
       74 +| Ref | Part | Value | Note |                                 
       75 +|-----|------|-------|------|                                 
       76 +| F1 | Fuse holder + fuse | 100mA 250V slow-blow | 5×20mm |

          +be |                                                          
       78 +| R1 | Resistor | 10kΩ 1W | fault current limit |

       83 +| C1 | Capacitor | 100nF 25V ceramic | bias filter |

       56 -## Stage 2 — 2.5V Bias + Input Divider                        
       91 +## Schematic 2 — NE5532 Comparator (U2, DIP-8)                
       92  
       58 -The 2.5V bias shifts the zero-volt reference to mid-rail, enab
          -ling the                                                      
       59 -single-supply MCU (0–5V ADC range) to measure both positive an
          -d negative voltages.

      119 +**Open-loop operation (no feedback):**                        
      120 +- BIAS_NODE > SAR_VOUT → CMP_OUT = +13V → 6N137 LED ON → PD2 =
          + LOW                                                          
      121 +- BIAS_NODE < SAR_VOUT → CMP_OUT = −13V → 6N137 LED OFF → PD2 
          += HIGH

          +pin 4 and GND.

       86 -## Stage 3 — NE5532 Comparator (safe side, ±15V supply)       
      127 +## Schematic 3 — 6N137 Digital Isolation (U3, DIP-8)          
      128  
       88 -NE5532 (DIP-8 dual op-amp) configured open-loop as SAR compara
          -tor.                                                          
       89 -Op-amp A is reserved for a differential amp (HCPL-7840 path if
          - added later).                                                
       90 -Op-amp B is the SAR comparator.                               
      129 +**Purpose:** Protects ATmega PD2 from comparator's ±13V swing 
          +and probe faults.                                             
      130  
      131  ```
       93 -NE5532 pinout (DIP-8):                                        
       94 -  pin 1 (OUT-A)  — reserved / NC                              
       95 -  pin 2 (IN−-A)  — reserved / NC                              
       96 -  pin 3 (IN+-A)  — reserved / NC                              
       97 -  pin 4 (V−)     — −15V                                       
       98 -  pin 5 (IN+-B)  — probe signal (from Stage 2)                
       99 -  pin 6 (IN−-B)  — 16-bit R-2R DAC VOUT (SAR reference)       
      100 -  pin 7 (OUT-B)  — comparator output → Stage 4 (6N137 drive)  
      101 -  pin 8 (V+)     — +15V                                       
      132 +CMP_OUT (U2 pin 7)                                            
      133 +     │                                                        
      134 +    [R4] 820Ω                                                 
      135 +     │                                                        
      136 +    [D3] 1N4148  anode→R4, cathode→U3 pin 2                   
      137 +     │                                                        
      138 +     │           6N137 (U3)                                   
      139 +     │          ┌──────────────────┐                          
      140 +     └── pin 2  │A  (LED anode)    │                          
      141 +                │K  (LED cathode)  │── pin 3 → GND            
      142 +                │                  │                          
      143 +                │  (isolation gap) │                          
      144 +                │                  │                          
      145 +     +5V ── pin 8 │VCC              │                         
      146 +     GND ── pin 7 │VE (enable)      │                         
      147 +                │Vo (output)        │── pin 6 ──[R5] 4.7kΩ── +
          +5V                                                            
      148 +                └──────────────────┘                │         
      149 +                                                    PD2_NET ──
          + U1 PD2 (pin 16)                                              
      150  ```
      151  
      104 -Bypass caps: 100nF ceramic between pin8→GND and pin4→GND close
          - to IC.                                                       
      105 -No feedback resistors — full open-loop gain gives sharp digita
          -l transitions.                                                
      152 +| Pin | Net | Notes |                                         
      153 +|-----|-----|-------|                                         
      154 +| 2 (anode) | via D3+R4 from CMP_OUT | LED drive input |      
      155 +| 3 (cathode) | GND | |                                       
      156 +| 6 (Vo, output) | PD2_NET | open-collector, pull-up to +5V vi
          +a R5 |                                                        
      157 +| 7 (VE, enable) | GND | tie low = always enabled |           
      158 +| 8 (VCC) | +5V | bypass: 100nF to GND |                      
      159  
      107 -NE5532-B output:                                              
      108 -- Signal > DAC: output → +13V (LED drive: ON)                 
      109 -- Signal < DAC: output → −13V (LED drive: OFF)

      115 -## Stage 4 — 6N137 Digital Isolation                          
      168 +## Schematic 4 — 16-bit SAR DAC (U4 + U5, 74HC595 DIP-16)     
      169  
      117 -Isolates the comparator output (which sees probe-referenced si
          -gnals) from ATmega PD2.                                       
      118 -Provides protection against probe faults, cable discharge, and
          - negative voltage transitions.                                
      170 +**Daisy-chain: U4 = high byte (D15–D8), U5 = low byte (D7–D0)*
          +*                                                             
      171  
      172  ```
      121 -NE5532-B out (pin7)                                           
      122 -  │                                                           
      123 -[R_led 820Ω]──[D_blk 1N4148 →]──────── 6N137 pin 2 (anode)    
      124 -                                        6N137 pin 3 (cathode) 
          -── GND                                                        
      125 -                                        6N137 pin 8 (VCC)     
          -── +5V                                                        
      126 -                                        6N137 pin 7 (VE)      
          -── GND                                                        
      127 -                    +5V ──[R_pu 4.7kΩ]──┬── 6N137 pin 6 (Vo)  
      128 -                                         └────────────────────
          -── ATmega PD2                                                 
      173 +ATmega32A                U4 (74HC595, IC1)              U5 (74
          +HC595, IC2)

          +───────────┐                                                  
      175 +PB5 (MOSI) ─────── DS  1│                │              │     
          +           │                                                  
      176 +PB7 (SCK)  ────── SRCLK 2│                │Q7' ─────── DS 1│

      131 -| NE5532-B out | D_blk state | 6N137 LED | PD2 |              
      132 -|-------------|-------------|-----------|-----|               
      133 -| +13V | forward | ON (~14mA) | LOW (0V, pulled by 6N137) |

      192 +**74HC595 detailed pin table:**                               
      193  
      136 -- R_led 820Ω: sets I_LED = (13 − 1.5) / 820 = 14mA ✓ (min 5mA,
          - max 25mA)                                                    
      137 -- D_blk 1N4148: blocks −13V from reaching 6N137 LED (reverse v
          -oltage protection)                                            
      138 -- R_pu 4.7kΩ: pull-up to +5V on open-collector output         
      139 -- Propagation delay: ~48ns — negligible at 10µs SAR settle tim
          -e per bit

      143 -## Stage 5 — 16-bit R-2R SAR DAC (2× 74HC595)                 
      217 +## Schematic 5 — 16-bit R-2R Ladder (SAR DAC)                 
      218  
      219 +16 bits → 16 shunt resistors (R = 10kΩ 1%) + 15 series resisto
          +rs + termination (2R = 20kΩ 1%).                              
      220 +                                                              
      221  ```
      146 -ATmega PB5 (MOSI) ── IC1-DS  (serial data)                    
      147 -ATmega PB7 (SCK)  ── IC1/IC2 SRCLK  (shift clock)             
      148 -ATmega PB3        ── IC1/IC2 RCLK   (latch — separate from PB4
          -)                                                             
      149 -IC1 Q7'           ── IC2 DS  (daisy chain: IC1 = high byte D15
          --D8)                                                          
      150 -IC1 Q7            ── VOUT  (MSB end of R-2R ladder)           
      151 -IC2 Q0            ──[20kΩ]── GND  (2R termination at LSB end) 
      222 +                                                      SAR_VOUT
          + (to U2 pin 6)                                                
      223 +                                                            │ 
      224 +U4-Q7(D15)──[Ra15=10k]──n15──[Rs14=10k]──n14──...──n1──[Rs0=10
          +k]──n0──[Rterm=20k]──GND                                      
      225 +                          │              │               │    
          +        │                                                     
      226 +                        [Rb15=20k]    [Rb14=20k]      [Rb1=20k
          +]   [Rb0=20k]                                                 
      227 +                          │              │               │    
          +        │                                                     
      228 +                         GND            GND             GND   
          +       GND                                                    
      229 +U4-Q6(D14)──[Ra14=10k]──n14                                   
      230 +U4-Q5(D13)──[Ra13=10k]──n13                                   
      231 +U4-Q4(D12)──[Ra12=10k]──n12                                   
      232 +U4-Q3(D11)──[Ra11=10k]──n11                                   
      233 +U4-Q2(D10)──[Ra10=10k]──n10                                   
      234 +U4-Q1(D9) ──[Ra9 =10k]──n9                                    
      235 +U4-Q0(D8) ──[Ra8 =10k]──n8                                    
      236 +U5-Q7(D7) ──[Ra7 =10k]──n7                                    
      237 +U5-Q6(D6) ──[Ra6 =10k]──n6                                    
      238 +U5-Q5(D5) ──[Ra5 =10k]──n5                                    
      239 +U5-Q4(D4) ──[Ra4 =10k]──n4                                    
      240 +U5-Q3(D3) ──[Ra3 =10k]──n3                                    
      241 +U5-Q2(D2) ──[Ra2 =10k]──n2                                    
      242 +U5-Q1(D1) ──[Ra1 =10k]──n1                                    
      243 +U5-Q0(D0) ──[Ra0 =10k]──n0                                    
      244  ```
      245  
      154 -R-2R ladder:                                                  
      155 -- R = 10kΩ (1%), 2R = 20kΩ (1%)                               
      156 -- 16 × 10kΩ shunt resistors + 17 × 20kΩ series + 20kΩ terminat
          -ion                                                           
      157 -- VOUT range: 0V to 5V (full supply)                          
      158 -- Resolution: 5V / 65536 = 76µV/step                          
      159 -- Effective bits with 1% resistors: ~8–10 bits (5–20mV practic
          -al resolution)                                                
      246 +**Resistor count:**                                           
      247 +- Ra0–Ra15: 16 × 10kΩ 1% (shunt arm — connects bit to node)   
      248 +- Rs0–Rs14: 15 × 10kΩ 1% (series spine between nodes)

      250 +- Rterm: 1 × 20kΩ 1% (termination at LSB end)

      161 -Bypass caps on each 595: 100nF ceramic between VCC (pin 16) an
          -d GND (pin 8).                                                
      253 +SAR_VOUT = n15 node = U4-Q7 junction.

      163 -Send high byte (D15–D8) first. IC1 receives first byte → holds
          - MSBs (D15=Q7).                                               
      164 -IC2 receives second byte → holds LSBs (D0=Q0).

      332 +## Schematic 8 — Power Connections                            
      333  
      212 -The 2.5V bias maps the full ±2.5V probe range onto the 0–5V co
          -mparator input:                                               
      213 -                                                              
      334  ```
      215 -probe  −2.5V → comparator pin5 ≈ 0.5V → SAR 0x0000            
      216 -probe   0V   → comparator pin5 = 2.5V → SAR 0x8000            
      217 -probe  +2.5V → comparator pin5 ≈ 4.5V → SAR 0xFFFF            
      218 -```                                                           
      335 ++15V rail ──── U2 pin 8 (NE5532 V+)     [bypass 100nF to GND] 
      336 +−15V rail ──── U2 pin 4 (NE5532 V−)     [bypass 100nF to GND] 
      337  
      220 -Firmware decodes: `millivolts = (int16_t)(raw − 0x8000) × 2500
          - / 32768`                                                     
      338 ++5V rail  ──── U1 pin 10 (ATmega VCC)   [bypass 100nF + 10µF t
          +o GND]                                                        
      339 +          ──── U1 pin 30 (ATmega AVCC)   [bypass 100nF + 10µF 
          +to GND]                                                       
      340 +          ──── U3 pin 8  (6N137 VCC)     [bypass 100nF to GND]
      341 +          ──── U4 pin 16 (74HC595 VCC)   [bypass 100nF to GND]
      342 +          ──── U5 pin 16 (74HC595 VCC)   [bypass 100nF to GND]
      343 +          ──── U4 pin 10 (/SRCLR = +5V, no clear)             
      344 +          ──── U5 pin 10 (/SRCLR = +5V)                       
      345 +          ──── R5 pull-up (4.7kΩ to 6N137 output)             
      346 +          ──── D1 clamp cathode                               
      347  
      222 -A result of `+1234` means +1.234V. A result of `-800` means −0
          -.800V.                                                        
      223 -No extra hardware needed — the bias is the mechanism.         
      348 +GND ──────── U1 pin 11, U3 pin 3, U3 pin 7                    
      349 +        ──── U4 pin 8, U5 pin 8                               
      350 +        ──── U4 pin 13 (/OE = GND, always enabled)            
      351 +        ──── U5 pin 13                                        
      352 +        ──── D2 clamp anode                                   
      353 +        ──── R3 (bias divider bottom)                         
      354 +        ──── C1 (bias filter)                                 
      355 +        ──── all R-2R termination resistors (Rterm, Rgt)      
      356 +        ──── all R-2R shunt resistors (Rb, Rh to GND)

      428 +## UART Commands (quick reference)                            
      429 +                                                              
      430 +| Command | Function | Output format |                        
      431 +|---------|----------|--------------|                         
      432 +| `f<hz>` | Set FG frequency (1–20000 Hz) | `OK f1000` |      
      433 +| `w<0-3>` | Set waveform 0=sin 1=sqr 2=tri 3=saw | `OK w0` | 
      434 +| `m0` | FG only mode (Timer2 @ 40kHz) | `OK mfg` |           
      435 +| `m1` | OSC only mode | `OK mosc` |                          
      436 +| `m2` | Both simultaneously (FG 10kHz max) | `OK mboth` |    
      437 +| `o` | Single SAR reading (16-bit) | `V: +1.234V` |          
      438 +| `os<n>` | Stream n SAR samples | `+1234\r\n` per line |     
      439 +| `oi` | Single internal ADC reading (10-bit) | `ADC: +1.234V`
          + |                                                            
      440 +| `oi<n>` | Stream n internal ADC samples | `+1234\r\n` per li
          +ne |                                                          
      441 +| `s` | Status report | `f1000 w0 mfg` |                      
      442 +                                                              
      443 +---                                                           
      444 +                                                              
      445 +## ADC Comparison (same probe input, same BIAS_NODE)          
      446 +                                                              
      447 +| Path | MCU pin | Resolution | Sample rate | Best use |      
      448 +|------|---------|-----------|-------------|---------|        
      449 +| Internal ADC | PA0 | 10-bit | ~38kSPS | Fast waveform shape 
          +|                                                             
      450 +| SAR ADC | PD2 (via 6N137) | 16-bit | ~6kSPS | Precise DC / s
          +low signals |                                                 
      451 +                                                              
      452 +Both decode with 2.5V bias:                                   
      453 +```                                                           
      454 +millivolts = (raw - mid) × V_fullscale / mid                  
      455 +Internal: mid = 511,  V_fullscale = 2500 mV                   
      456 +SAR:      mid = 0x8000, V_fullscale = 2500 mV                 
      457 +```                                                           
      458 +

  Good on the 4 commands. Quick summary:
  - o / os<n> → SAR (16-bit, slow, precise, via 6N137+PD2)
  - oi / oi<n> → internal ADC (10-bit, fast ~38kSPS, via PA0 directly)

● Quick test plan: wire the 595 chain → build R-2R → send d<hex> over UART →
  measure with multimeter. I'll add a d<hex> test command too.

  ---
  Phase 0 Wiring — 2× 74HC595 + ATmega32A SPI

  ATmega32A (DIP-40)               IC1 (74HC595, high byte)
                                    ┌──────────────┐
  PB5 MOSI  pin 6 ────────────────►│14  DS        │
  PB7 SCK   pin 8 ────────┬───────►│11  SRCLK     │
  PB3 latch pin 4 ──────┬─┼───────►│12  RCLK      │
                         │ │        │               │
  +5V ─────────────────┐ │ │  +5V─►│16  VCC       │  100nF cap: VCC→GND
  GND ─────────────────┤ │ │  GND─►│ 8  GND       │
                       │ │ │  +5V─►│10  /SRCLR    │  (tie HIGH = no clear)
                       │ │ │  GND─►│13  /OE       │  (tie LOW  = always on)
                       │ │ │        │               │
                       │ │ │        │    QH' pin 9 ─┼──┐  daisy chain
                       │ │ │        │               │  │
                       │ │ │    IC2 (74HC595, low byte) │
                       │ │ │        ┌──────────────┐  │
                       │ └─┼───────►│11  SRCLK     │  │
                       │   └───────►│12  RCLK      │  │
                       │            │               │  │
                       │       +5V─►│16  VCC       │  │
                       │       GND─►│ 8  GND       │  │
                       │       +5V─►│10  /SRCLR    │  │
                       │       GND─►│13  /OE       │  │
                       │            │               │  │
                       └────────────┘   DS  pin 14 ◄┘  ← IC1 QH' → IC2 DS

  74HC595 output → DAC bit mapping:

  ┌─────┬─────┬──────┬─────┬──────────────────────┐
  │ IC  │ Pin │ Name │ Bit │         DAC          │

  ├─────┼─────┼──────┼─────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ IC2 │ 1   │ QB   │ Q1  │ D1                   │
  ├─────┼─────┼──────┼─────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ IC2 │ 15  │ QA   │ Q0  │ D0 LSB → termination │
  └─────┴─────┴──────┴─────┴──────────────────────┘

  ---
  Phase 0 R-2R Ladder

  VOUT (measure here with multimeter)
    │
    │← IC1 pin7 (D15, MSB)
    │
   [10k]──node15──[10k]──node14──[10k]──node13── ...
  ──node1──[10k]──node0──[20k]──GND
             │             │             │                        │            │
           [20k]         [20k]         [20k]                   [20k]
  [20k]
             │             │             │                        │            │
           IC1p7         IC1p6         IC1p5                   IC2p1
  IC2p15(D0)
           (D15)         (D14)         (D13)                   (D1)
  termination end

  Each node nX:
    - one 10k SERIES resistor to the right (spine toward LSB)
    - one 20k SHUNT resistor down to GND
    - one bit line coming in via 10k (the Ra resistors from the 595 output)

  Full count: 31 × 10kΩ 1% + 17 × 20kΩ 1%

  ---
  Test Command d<hex> — add to cmd.h

  Open src/drivers/cmd.h and add one block inside _cmd_exec(), right before the
  final else:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Clean build. Now the test procedure:

  ---
  Phase 0 Test Procedure

  Wire breadboard: ATmega → IC1 → IC2 → R-2R ladder. Power up, open serial at
  9600.

  ┌───────┬───────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Send  │ Expected VOUT │           What it checks            │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ d0000 │ ~0.0 V        │ LSB end (all zeros)                 │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ d8000 │ ~2.5 V        │ midpoint — same as power-on default │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ dffff │ ~5.0 V        │ all bits set                        │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ d4000 │ ~1.25 V       │ MSB of low byte                     │
  ├───────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ dc000 │ ~3.75 V       │ 2 MSBs set                          │
  └───────┴───────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

  Measure VOUT with multimeter between the IC1 pin 7 / n15 node and GND.

  If voltages are roughly correct (within ±0.1V), the 595 chain and R-2R are
  working. Then you can add the NE5532 and 6N137 for full SAR operation.

  ▎ Tip: If d8000 reads ~0V instead of ~2.5V, the byte order is swapped — means 
  ▎ IC1/IC2 are physically reversed. Swap their DS connections.

  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Check       │                    What to verify                    │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC1 pin 10       │ Must be tied to +5V (if GND → outputs always cleared │
  │ /SRCLR           │  = 0V)                                               │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC1 pin 13 /OE   │ Must be tied to GND (if floating → outputs hi-Z)     │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC2 same         │ Both pins 10 and 13 same as IC1                      │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC1 pin 14 DS    │ Connected to ATmega PB5 (pin 6 on DIP-40)            │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC1 pin 11 SRCLK │ Connected to ATmega PB7 (pin 8)                      │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC1 pin 12 RCLK  │ Connected to ATmega PB3 (pin 4)                      │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC1 pin 9 QH'    │ Connected to IC2 pin 14 DS                           │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IC2 SRCLK/RCLK   │ Also connected to PB7/PB3                            │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ VCC/GND          │ Both ICs pin 16 = +5V, pin 8 = GND                   │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The /SRCLR being low is the most common breadboard mistake — outputs stay at 0
  no matter what you shift in.

      21 -        dac16_write(0xFFFF);                                   
      22 -        _delay_ms(500);                                        
      23 -                                                               
      24 -        PORTD &= ~(1 << PD5);   /* LED off */                  
      25 -        dac16_write(0x0000);                                   
      26 -        _delay_ms(500);                                        
      19 +        dac16_write((uint16_t)pgm_read_byte(&sine_table[idx]) <
         +< 8);                                                          
      20 +        _delay_us(4);                                          
      21 +        idx++;                                                 
      22      }
      23  }

❯ what /SS measn 

● Flash it — scope should show ~1kHz sine on VOUT.

  ---
  /SS = Slave Select, active-LOW. ATmega32A rule: if PB4 (/SS) is left as an
  input and the pin goes LOW (floating = random), the hardware SPI automatically
  switches the chip from master to slave — and stops transmitting. You'd be
  writing to SPDR but nothing shifts out.

  Fix: set PB4 as output in dac16_init(). Then the hardware ignores the pin
  state and stays in master mode permanently. That one line was why the DAC did
  nothing.

✻ Sautéed for 18s

❯ hey one issue for the adc all perfect resistors but for the voltage ofset     
  shop only got 22k and all 5% for now later we cnq interpolate a plot right?   

● Yes, absolutely. A calibration lookup table with linear interpolation is the
  standard fix for exactly this — non-ideal resistor ratios and 5% tolerance
  both get absorbed into it.

  How it works:

  1. Write known codes to the DAC (e.g. every 4096 steps = 16 points across
  0–65535)
  2. Measure actual VOUT at each point with a good multimeter
  3. Store the table in firmware: code → measured_mV
  4. For any SAR result between two table points, interpolate:

  mV = table[i] + (table[i+1] - table[i]) * (code - code[i]) / (code[i+1] -
  code[i])

  Why it works for the SAR specifically:

  The SAR finds the DAC code that equals the input. If you know what voltage
  that code actually produces (from calibration), you know the input voltage —
  non-linearity cancels out completely.

  For now: wire it up with 22kΩ 5%, run it, and the readings will be roughly
  correct. When you get 20kΩ 1% resistors, swap them out and the error drops to
  near zero without needing calibration at all.

  The 22kΩ shifts your full-scale output slightly (ratio 2.2 instead of 2.0) but
  the SAR will still converge — it just needs the calibration table to give
  accurate millivolt readings.
✻ Cooked for 30s

❯ since that the offset havign mix parts also doent matter right?               

● Correct — with calibration it doesn't matter at all. The table maps code →
  actual voltage regardless of what caused the non-linearity: wrong ratio, mixed
  values, 5% spread, all of it gets absorbed into the same calibration in one
  shot.

  The only thing that must hold is monotonicity — DAC output must never go down
  when the code goes up, otherwise the SAR binary search gets confused and
  converges to the wrong answer. With a proper R-2R layout this is almost
  impossible to break even with mixed resistors, because each bit's contribution
  is still positive.

  So use whatever you have now, calibrate, get good readings. Swap in matched 1%
  parts later if you want and just re-run the calibration.

❯ my circuit has ampifiers right /doe they have a cass based categorization?    

● Yes — amplifier classes apply, though for op-amps it's less commonly talked
  about.

  Your NE5532 internally:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │       Stage        │ Class  │                    Why                    │
  ├────────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Input differential │ Class  │ Both transistors always conducting, full  │
  │  pair              │ A      │ cycle                                     │
  ├────────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Output stage       │ Class  │ Push-pull, small quiescent current to     │
  │                    │ AB     │ avoid crossover distortion                │
  └────────────────────┴────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The class system summarized:

  ┌───────┬────────────────────┬────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
  │ Class │  Conduction angle  │ Efficiency │           Used for            │
  ├───────┼────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ A     │ 360° (always on)   │ ~25%       │ Low-noise preamps, op-amp     │
  │       │                    │            │ inputs                        │
  ├───────┼────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ B     │ 180° (half cycle   │ ~78%       │ Rarely alone — bad crossover  │
  │       │ each)              │            │ distortion                    │
  ├───────┼────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ AB    │ slightly >180°     │ ~60-70%    │ Most audio amps, op-amp       │
  │       │                    │            │ outputs                       │
  ├───────┼────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ C     │ <180°              │ >90%       │ RF/tuned circuits only — very │
  │       │                    │            │  distorted                    │
  ├───────┼────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ D     │ switching (PWM)    │ ~95%       │ Class D audio amps, motor     │
  │       │                    │            │ drivers                       │
  └───────┴────────────────────┴────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

  In your circuit specifically:

  - NE5532-B is wired open-loop as a comparator — so the class classification is
  almost irrelevant here. It's not amplifying linearly, just switching output
  rail-to-rail. The 6N137 only cares about whether the output is HIGH or LOW.

  For your campus report: NE5532 is a Class AB op-amp, used here as a comparator
  (open-loop, no feedback).

✻ Cooked for 26s

❯ liek class A, B, AB, C                                                        

● Yes, those are the main ones. Quick breakdown:

  Class A — output transistor conducts the full 360° of the input cycle
  - Always on, never cuts off
  - Most linear, lowest distortion
  - Very inefficient (~25%) — wastes power as heat even with no signal
  - Example: NE5532 input stage, audiophile preamps

  Class B — two transistors, each conducts 180° (one for + half, one for − half)
  - Much more efficient (~78%)
  - Problem: crossover distortion at the zero-crossing where one transistor
  hands off to the other
  - Rarely used alone

  Class AB — each transistor conducts slightly more than 180°
  - Small quiescent bias current keeps both transistors barely on at the
  crossover point
  - Fixes Class B's crossover distortion
  - Good efficiency + low distortion tradeoff
  - Most common: NE5532 output stage, audio power amps

  Class C — transistor conducts less than 180° (less than half the cycle)
  - Very high efficiency (>90%)
  - Extremely distorted — only works with a tuned LC tank circuit to reconstruct
  the sine
  - Only used in RF transmitters, never audio

  Class D — not about conduction angle at all — output switches fully ON or OFF
  (PWM)
  - ~95% efficient
  - Used in modern audio amps, motor drivers, your ATmega Timer PWM output is
  technically Class D

  Your NE5532 = Class AB. Used as a comparator in your circuit so it operates
  more like Class C — hard switching, not linear at all.

✻ Brewed for 1m 38s

❯ telle me about the ampifier class and the power                               

❯ or is that namign useless in mine                                             

● Honestly — useless in your circuit. Here's why:

  Amplifier classes (A/B/AB/C/D) describe power efficiency — what fraction of
  the cycle the output transistors conduct. That only matters when you're
  delivering watts to a load (speakers, motors, RF transmitters).

  Your circuit is entirely signal-level processing — µW to mW. At that level:

  ┌──────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┬────────────┐
  │           Your amp           │    What actually matters    │   Class?   │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ TL071 U28 (input buffer)     │ Input impedance, noise,     │ Irrelevant │
  │                              │ bandwidth                   │            │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ TL071 U16 (summing amp)      │ Offset voltage, gain        │ Irrelevant │
  │                              │ accuracy                    │            │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ TL071 U9/U7 (gain ranging)   │ Slew rate, bandwidth,       │ Irrelevant │
  │                              │ linearity                   │            │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ NE5532 U6A/U6B (Sallen-Key   │ Bandwidth, low noise        │ Irrelevant │
  │ filter)                      │                             │            │
  └──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┴────────────┘

  Nobody in signal processing talks about class. The specs that matter for your
  design are:

  - Bandwidth — TL071: 3MHz, NE5532: 10MHz ✓ both fine for your signal range
  - Slew rate — TL071: 13V/µs, NE5532: 9V/µs ✓ fine for kHz signals
  - Noise — NE5532 is significantly quieter than TL071 (why you used it for the
  filters)
  - Supply voltage — both work on ±15V ✓

  The class system is a power amp concept. Your circuit doesn't need it.

✻ Baked for 30s

❯ so in the pin in the HC-05 TX pin it need a voltag edivider right? wha abotu  
  i use 22k and 10 instead of 1k and 2k                                         

● Depends on orientation — wrong way round gives wrong voltage:

  Option A — 10kΩ series, 22kΩ to GND:
  ATmega TX (5V) →[10k]→ HC-05 RX
                             │
                           [22k]
                             │
                            GND

  Vout = 5 × 22/(10+22) = 3.44V  ✓  (just within HC-05's 3.6V max)

  Option B — 22kΩ series, 10kΩ to GND:
  Vout = 5 × 10/32 = 1.56V  ✗  too low, HC-05 won't read as HIGH

  So Option A works — 10kΩ series, 22kΩ to GND. 3.44V is slightly above 3.3V but
  within the HC-05 absolute max of 3.6V. Fine for 9600 baud.

  The higher resistance values (vs standard 1k+2k) are no problem at 9600 baud —
  bit period is 104µs and the RC delay is negligible.

✻ Cogitated for 1m 6s

❯ ny alternative 10 k and 5.6k                                                  

● 5.6kΩ series, 10kΩ to GND:

  ATmega TX (5V) →[5.6k]→ HC-05 RX
                               │
                             [10k]
                               │
                              GND

  Vout = 5 × 10/(5.6+10) = 3.2V  ✓  near perfect

  Better than the 22k+10k option — 3.2V is right on target for 3.3V logic. Use
  this one.

❯ shoil we seprate 15 v and 8v to seperate lines or keep lek thsi okayu? [Image 
  #13]                                                                          
  ⎿  [Image #13]

● Keep them separate — they already are and that's correct.

  Why separate:
  - ±15V → NE5532, TL071 op-amps (need headroom above signal swing)
  - ±8V → CD4051B, CD4066BE analog switches (lower supply, less noise into
  analog paths)
  - +5V → ATmega32A, 74HC595 (digital, separate from analog rails)

  Mixing ±15V and ±8V on the same net would be a short.

  The shared +24V input bus is fine — both regulator pairs drawing from the same
  ±24V input is standard practice. No issue there.

  One thing to check: your 1N4007 diodes (D3, D4, D7, D8) on the regulator
  outputs — those are reverse-protection diodes. Make sure they're oriented
  correctly (anode toward regulator output, cathode toward load). If backwards
  they'll block the supply entirely.
✻ Baked for 46s

❯ currtly 8V comes from 15 volts dont you see? [Image #14]                      
  ⎿  [Image #14]

● Ah yes, I see now — cascaded: ±24V → L7815/L7915 → ±15V → L7808/L7908 → ±8V.

  This is actually a smart design. Keep it.

  Why cascaded is better here:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  │                    │ Cascaded (current) │ Direct from ±24V │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ L7808 voltage drop │ 15V − 8V = 7V      │ 24V − 8V = 16V   │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ L7808 heat         │ Low                │ Much higher      │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ Works?             │ ✓                  │ ✓                │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────────┴──────────────────┘

  Less voltage drop across L7808 = less heat = less heatsink needed.

  One thing to watch: L7815 must supply current for both the ±15V loads (NE5532,
  TL071) AND the ±8V loads (CD4051B, CD4066). Check that the combined current
  stays within L7815's 1A limit. Should be fine since op-amps and analog
  switches draw very little.

✻ Brewed for 41s

❯ so is thsi okay for 5V [Image #15]                                            
  ⎿  [Image #15]

● Yes, looks correct. Standard LM2596T-5 buck converter circuit.

  What I see:
  - VIN = +24V2 ✓
  - ON/OFF (pin 5) → GND = always enabled ✓
  - D5 (1N5822 Schottky) = catch diode ✓ good choice
  - L2 (33µH 3A) = output inductor ✓
  - C22 (220µF) = output cap ✓
  - FB (pin 4) → output = correct for fixed 5V version ✓

  One minor note: LM2596 datasheet recommends ~68µH for 24V→5V at full 3A load.
  Your 33µH will work but output ripple will be slightly higher. For your load
  (ATmega + 595s + HC-05 = well under 1A) it's absolutely fine — ripple won't be
  a problem at light loads.

  Everything else is good. This is a clean 5V supply.

● Those are KiCad text label types:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
  │       Symbol       │      Name      │               Scope               │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A                  │ Plain text     │ Just a visual label, no           │
  │                    │                │ electrical connection             │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A (with flag)      │ Net label      │ Connects all same-named nets on   │
  │                    │                │ same sheet                        │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A (box)            │ Global label   │ Connects same-named nets across   │
  │                    │                │ all sheets                        │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A→                 │ Hierarchical   │ Connects between parent/child     │
  │                    │ label          │ sheet pairs                       │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A                  │                │ Special net — globally connected, │
  │ (highlighted/blue) │ Power symbol   │  shown on every sheet             │
  │                    │                │ automatically                     │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘

  For your power rails (+5V, +15V, GND etc.) — you're already using power 
  symbols (the blue one), which is correct.

  ---
  Regarding your external PSU: if you're feeding from a good bench/metal PSU,
  the LM2596 and 78xx regulators are still useful — they give you clean stable
  voltages isolated from whatever the PSU does. Keep them in the design.

❯ so thsi is my 16 bit 2-2R ladder can we liek make a seperate symbol or liek   
  fgroup so when i onyl want to edti ti o go ti its internal circuti since      
  these are gettign larger and larger [Image #17]                               
  ⎿  [Image #17]

● Yes — use a hierarchical sheet. That's exactly what it's for.

  How to do it in KiCad:

  1. Place → Hierarchical Sheet on your main schematic
  2. Give it a name like DAC16_R2R
  3. Double-click it → opens the sub-sheet
  4. Move your 2× 74HC595 + entire R-2R ladder into that sub-sheet
  5. Add Hierarchical Labels for the pins that cross the boundary:
    - SER (PB1 in)
    - SRCLK (PB2 in)
    - RCLK (PB3 in? — check your actual pins)
    - VOUT (to NE5532 comparator)
    - +5V, GND
  6. On the parent sheet, those labels become the sheet's pins — connect them to
  your ATmega buses

  Result:
  Main sheet:
    ATmega ──[DAC16_R2R]── VOUT → comparator

  Double-click [DAC16_R2R] → see full 595 + R-2R ladder inside

  Same idea for the programmable gain amp (TL071 + CD4066) — that block is also
  growing large. You could have:
  - DAC16_R2R.kicad_sch
  - GAIN_AMP.kicad_sch
  - POWER.kicad_sch

  Keeps the top-level schematic clean and readable.

✻ Sautéed for 45s

❯ so hwo to add pins [Image #19]                                                
  ⎿  [Image #19]

● You're inside the sub-sheet (DAC16_R2R page 2) already. To add pins that
  appear on the sheet boundary:

  Place → Hierarchical Label (shortcut L)

  1. Press L → place a hierarchical label
  2. Type the net name e.g. SER
  3. Set the type: Input / Output / Bidirectional / Power In / Power Out
  4. Connect it to the wire going to 595 SER pin

  Labels you need here:

  ┌───────┬──────────┬─────────────────┐
  │ Label │   Type   │   Connects to   │
  ├───────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ SER   │ Input    │ U29 pin 14 (DS) │
  ├───────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ SRCLK │ Input    │ U29/U30 pin 11  │
  ├───────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ RCLK  │ Input    │ U29/U30 pin 12  │
  ├───────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ VOUT  │ Output   │ R-2R MSB end    │
  ├───────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ +5V   │ Power In │ VCC rails       │
  ├───────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ GND   │ Power In │ GND rails       │
  └───────┴──────────┴─────────────────┘

  Then go back to page 1 — the DAC16_R2R sheet box will show those labels as
  pins on its border. Connect them to your ATmega PB nets there.

  +5V and GND don't actually need hierarchical labels since they're global power
  symbols — they connect automatically across all sheets.

✻ Worked for 42s

❯ are the chip name global liek U29 or we can just set reset liek U1 [Image     
  #22]                                                                          
  ⎿  [Image #22]

● Reference designators (U1, U29 etc.) are global across the entire project — no
  two components can share the same reference.

  So U29 on page 2 and U1 on page 1 are different components. You can't have two
  U1s anywhere in the project.

  To renumber: Tools → Annotate Schematic → this automatically reassigns all
  references in order across all sheets. It will rename U29 → U1 (or whatever
  the next free number is) consistently.

  Most people just run annotate at the end when the schematic is complete rather
  than worrying about numbers during design.

❯ also what abotu this [Image #23]

● This is the "Synchronize sheet pins and hierarchical labels" dialog.

  - Left column (DAC16_R2R) — pins on the sheet symbol in the parent (page 1)
  - Middle column (untitled.kicad_sch) — hierarchical labels inside the
  sub-sheet (page 2)
  - Right column — matched pairs

  Right now all 4 labels (RCLK, SER, SRCLK, VOUT) are in the middle column only
  — meaning they exist inside the sub-sheet but not yet as pins on the sheet 
  symbol.

  Click the middle arrow (←) to import them → moves all 4 to the right column =
  creates matching sheet pins on the parent sheet symbol.

  Then Close → go to page 1 → your DAC16_R2R box will have RCLK, SER, SRCLK,
  VOUT pins on it ready to connect to the ATmega.

✻ Crunched for 40s
❯ also clearly see the circt where VOUT is fed thsi is an invertign op amp      
  earler is was consfere for DAC0808 and this si their recomnded way but now    
  its a resisotr netwek n dsee its has the feedback resisotr 3.3k but no R1 but 
  in case it will be the resulting resistor from the resisot rnewtk             
  mathematicaly find if its okay [Image #24] or need to put seperate voltage    
  follower then invert                                                          
  ⎿  [Image #24]

● Good question. The R-2R Thevenin resistance is R1 — no separate voltage
  follower needed. Here's the math:

  Thevenin resistance of R-2R ladder = R (series resistor value), always, 
  regardless of bit code.

  So the circuit is equivalent to:

  V_th (R-2R open circuit voltage)
      │
    [R_th = R_series]
      │
      ├──── pin 2 (virtual ground)
      │
     [R7 = 3.3k] feedback
      │
     Vout = −V_th × (3.3k / R_series)

  To confirm the gain matches your note (0 to −6.6V):

  ┌──────────┬───────┬───────────────────────────┐
  │ R_series │ Gain  │ Output range (0–5V input) │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ 1kΩ      │ −3.3  │ 0 to −16.5V (clipped)     │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ 2.5kΩ    │ −1.32 │ 0 to −6.6V ✓              │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ 3.3kΩ    │ −1.0  │ 0 to −5V                  │
  ├──────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ 10kΩ     │ −0.33 │ 0 to −1.65V               │
  └──────────┴───────┴───────────────────────────┘

  Your note says 0 to −6.6V → R_series on PORTC R-2R should be ~2.5kΩ. What
  value resistors did you use for the function generator R-2R on PORTC? That
  confirms whether R7=3.3k is correct.

  C47 (47pF across R7) — correct, this is a stability compensation cap. Cuts
  gain at high frequency, prevents TL071 oscillation. Keep it.

❯ R = 10k, 2R = 22k 1% resistors                                                
  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

❯ yo you already knwo these                                                     

● Right, same resistors as the 16-bit DAC — 10kΩ and 22kΩ 1%.

  So with R_th = 10kΩ and Rf = 3.3kΩ:

  Gain = −3.3k/10k = −0.33

  Output range = 0 to −0.33 × 5V = 0 to −1.65V

  That doesn't match your note "0 to −6.6V" — the note is leftover from the
  DAC0808 design. You need to change R7.

  For 0 to −6.6V output:
  - Rf = 6.6/5 × 10k = 13.2kΩ → use 12kΩ + 1.2kΩ in series, or just a 13kΩ

  For 0 to −5V (cleaner number):
  - Rf = 5/5 × 10k = 10kΩ → unity gain inverter, simple

  What output range do you actually want?

❯ [Image #25] see nexe

● Now I can see the full chain. Let me trace it:

  U7 (gain = −0.33) → CD4066 switches (R21/R22/R23/R24) → U20 (Rf=R25=10k,
  "Inverting Post-Amp")

  Combined gain for each range:

  ┌────────┬──────┬──────────┬────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  │ Switch │ R_in │ U20 gain │ Total (×−0.33) │ Output (0–5V in) │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ U17A   │ 5.6k │ −1.79    │ +0.59          │ 0→+2.95V         │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ U17B   │ 56k  │ −0.18    │ +0.059         │ 0→+0.295V        │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ U17C   │ 560k │ −0.018   │ +0.006         │ 0→+0.030V        │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ U17D   │ 5.6M │ −0.0018  │ +0.0006        │ tiny             │
  └────────┴──────┴──────────┴────────────────┴──────────────────┘

  Two inversions → output is positive. The note "0 to −6.6V" is stale from the
  DAC0808 era — doesn't apply anymore with 10kΩ R-2R Thevenin.

  Your options:
  1. Update R7 to get the range you want at U7 output
  2. Keep as is — the CD4066 ranges give you 4 programmable amplitude levels for
  the FG output, which is actually useful

  What's the intended final output range for the function generator?

❯ /model                                                                        
  ⎿  Set model to Opus 4.8 (1M context) and saved as your default for new
     sessions

❯ then next [Image #25]                                                         
  ⎿  [Image #25]

● Now I can see the output filter stage. Let me trace this fully:

  Summing Amp (TL071 U16):
  - V_SIGNAL → R20 (4.7k) → pin 2
  - V_OFFSET → R17 (4.7k) → pin 2
  - Rf = R15 (4.7k), C48 (47pF) compensation
  - Gain = −4.7k/4.7k = −1 for each input
  - Output = −(V_SIGNAL + V_OFFSET) → splits into two paths: MUX_A0 and MUX_A1

  Two Sallen-Key Low-Pass Filters (4-pole total = 2× 2-pole):

  ┌─────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────┬───────────┐
  │ Filter  │    Op-amp    │      R       │          C           │ Cutoff fc │
  ├─────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────┤
  │ Path A1 │ U6B (NE5532) │ R19,R8 = 10k │ C23=2.7nF, C21=470pF │ ~4.5kHz   │
  ├─────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────┤
  │ Path A0 │ U6A (NE5532) │ R6,R18 = 10k │ C16=1nF, C19=1nF     │ ~16kHz    │
  └─────────┴──────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────┴───────────┘

  Wait — these are two different cutoff frequencies, not cascaded. They go to
  separate MUX inputs (MUX_A0, MUX_A1). So the CD4051 selects which filter
  (which bandwidth) is applied — probably switchable filtering depending on 
  output frequency range.

  The "0V to −9.4V (all negative)" note:

  This is the issue. The summing amp inverts, so a 0→+5V DDS input becomes
  0→−5V. With V_OFFSET added you get the −9.4V range. The output is all negative
  — which is correct for the next inverting post-amp stage (U20) that flips it
  back positive.

  Question to confirm the design intent: Is the signal chain meant to be:

  DDS(PORTC) → U7 inv → CD4066 gain → U20 inv → ???

  vs

  DDS → Summing amp U16 → Sallen-Key filter → MUX → output

  These look like two separate signal paths. Which one is the actual FG output
  to the BNC? Or does one feed the other?

● Now I have the complete picture from the netlist. Let me give you real
  engineering answers — and I found some wiring gaps you need to know about.

  1. Voltage follower vs. R-2R direct — definitive answer

  No follower needed. Feeding the R-2R straight into the inverting input is not 
  a compromise — it's the better method.

  An R-2R ladder has a constant output resistance = R (your 10k), regardless of 
  the digital code. When you connect its output to the op-amp's inverting input
  (a virtual ground at 0V), it becomes a current-mode DAC — exactly how the
  DAC0808 works internally.

  R-2R output  ──[Rth = 10k]──┬──[Rf]──┐
  (Thevenin: Voc, 10k)        │        │
                           (virtual    └──→ Vout
                            ground 0V)
          Vout = −(Rf / 10k) × Voc

  - The virtual ground forces the ladder output to 0V → current = Voc/10k flows
  into Rf
  - Output is perfectly linear in the DAC code
  - The R7 = 3.3k you have IS Rf; the "missing R1" IS the ladder's 10k Thevenin.
  Your instinct was right.

  A voltage follower would actually hurt — it adds offset/noise and buys you
  nothing, because the inverting input already presents an ideal load.

  Gain = −Rf / 10k. That's the whole story for the input stage.

  2. Your current resistor values give attenuation, not ±15V

  I traced the signal chain in the netlist:

  DAC → U7(inv, ×−0.33) → CD4066 select → U20(inv, gain) → V_SIGNAL
                                            Rf2=R25=10k

  The four CD4066-selected input resistors on U20 are R21=5.6k, R22=56k, 
  R23=560k, R24=5.6M. That gives U20 gains of:

  ┌──────────┬──────────┬──────────────┬─────────┬─────────────────┐
  │  Switch  │ U20 gain │ × U7 (−0.33) │  Total  │ Max out (Vs=5V) │
  ├──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ R21 5.6k │ −1.79    │              │ 0.59    │ 2.95V           │
  ├──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ R22 56k  │ −0.18    │              │ 0.059   │ 0.30V           │
  ├──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ R23 560k │ −0.018   │              │ 0.0059  │ 0.03V           │
  ├──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┼─────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ R24 5.6M │ −0.0018  │              │ 0.00059 │ 0.003V          │
  └──────────┴──────────┴──────────────┴─────────┴─────────────────┘

  So today the chain maxes at ~3V and then attenuates ×10, ×100, ×1000. You can
  never reach ±15V with these values. This is an attenuator bank, not an
  amplifier bank.

  To reach ±15V, you need the signal first centered to ±2.5V, then a gain of ~6.
  Recommended:

  - Center the signal at U7: add a 20kΩ resistor from +5V into U7's inverting
  node alongside the DAC. With Rf7 = 10k: Vout7 = −(Vs−2.5) → ±2.5V swing.
  - Gain ranges at U20 (Rf2 = 60k, vary input R) for ×1/×2/×4/×6:

  ┌───────┬─────┬──────┬───────────────────┐
  │ Range │ Rin │ Gain │ Output from ±2.5V │
  ├───────┼─────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
  │ ×1    │ 60k │ 1    │ ±2.5V             │
  ├───────┼─────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
  │ ×2    │ 30k │ 2    │ ±5V               │
  ├───────┼─────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
  │ ×4    │ 15k │ 4    │ ±10V              │
  ├───────┼─────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
  │ ×6    │ 10k │ 6    │ ±15V              │
  └───────┴─────┴──────┴───────────────────┘

  3. How offset gives your 3 modes

  Once the signal is symmetric (±A) out of the gain stage, the offset DAC (via
  its own 10k Thevenin into the final summing node) just adds a DC level:

  ┌───────────────┬────────────────┬─────────┐
  │     Mode      │ Offset setting │ Output  │
  ├───────────────┼────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ Centered      │ 0V             │ −A … +A │
  ├───────────────┼────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ Full positive │ +A             │ 0 … +2A │
  ├───────────────┼────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ Full negative │ −A             │ −2A … 0 │
  └───────────────┴────────────────┴─────────┘

  Constraint you correctly intuited: max offset = current amplitude A, and at
  full offset the peak (2A) must stay ≤ ~13.5V (TL071 swing on ±15V). So highest
  offset is only available at lower amplitudes — exactly your "min amplitude in
  +V region" reasoning.

  4. Critical wiring gaps I found in your netlist ⚠️

  These will make it not work until fixed:

  1. DAC16 control pins are not connected to the MCU. /DAC16_R2R/SER, SRCLK,
  RCLK have no ATmega node. PB3, PB4, PB5, PB7 are all listed unconnected. Your
  firmware (dac16.h) uses SER←PB5 (MOSI), SRCLK←PB7 (SCK), RCLK←PB3. Wire those
  three on the parent sheet.
  2. DAC16 VOUT is dangling. Net /DAC16_R2R/VOUT connects only to R64 pin 1 — it
  never reaches U7. Connect VOUT → U7 pin 2.
  3. Only ONE 16-bit DAC exists. The offset-path amps (U9→U21→U22) have no DAC
  source — U9 pin 2 has feedback only. You need to place the second R-2R DAC to
  drive U9.
  4. PORTC (PC0–PC7) is unconnected — leftover from the old 8-bit DAC0808 plan.
  Fine to leave; you've moved to the 16-bit 595 DAC.

  ---
  Want me to:
  - (A) give exact resistor values + the centering injection for the full ±15V
  chain (signal + offset), or
  - (B) first help you wire the DAC16 control lines and VOUT so you can
  bench-test the basic 16-bit output, then do the amp math?

  Which order?

● CLAUDE.md is written. Now your amplifier questions.

  What to set R7 (Rf of U7) to

  Right now U7 only attenuates (×−0.33), which throws away DAC resolution. Set
  U7 to unity gain and put the real gain in the post-amp stage.

  - R7 = 10k → U7 gain = −10k/10k = −1.0

  That keeps the full 0–5V swing through stage 1, and lets U20 do all the
  amplification cleanly.

  Your 8 gain resistors — linear (×10) vs binary

  You said you'll have 8 CD4066 switches (two CD4066 chips). The key design
  question: how should the 8 steps be spaced?

  Your current values (5.6k, 56k, 560k, 5.6M) step by ×10 each — that's only
  useful for a decade attenuator (×1, ×0.1, ×0.01…), not amplitude control. Two
  problems:
  1. They go the wrong way (attenuate, not amplify)
  2. ×10 jumps are huge — no fine control

  Better: linear amplitude steps. With Rf2 = 60k fixed on U20, pick input
  resistors for evenly-spaced gain:

  ┌────────┬──────┬─────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
  │ Switch │ Rin  │ Gain (−60k/Rin) │ Output from ±2.5V signal │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 1      │ 60k  │ ×1              │ ±2.5V                    │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 2      │ 30k  │ ×2              │ ±5V                      │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 3      │ 20k  │ ×3              │ ±7.5V                    │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 4      │ 15k  │ ×4              │ ±10V                     │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 5      │ 12k  │ ×5              │ ±12.5V                   │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 6      │ 10k  │ ×6              │ ±15V (max)               │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 7      │ 8.6k │ ×7              │ clips                    │
  ├────────┼──────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 8      │ 7.5k │ ×8              │ clips                    │
  └────────┴──────┴─────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

  That gives you even 2.5V steps from ±2.5V up to ±15V — much more usable than
  ×10 jumps. Steps 7–8 clip on ±15V rails, so realistically use 6 ranges.

  "Increment 10 by 10" — your real question

  I think you're asking: should the output voltage step in nice round amounts 
  (like 10mV, 100mV) or just whatever the DAC gives?

  - With 16-bit DAC: 5V/65536 = 76µV steps. You can hit any round number (10mV,
  100mV) almost exactly by software — pick the nearest code. Round increments 
  are easy.
  - Without 16-bit (8-bit only): 5V/256 = 19.5mV steps. You cannot land on
  exactly 10mV — nearest is 19.5mV or 39mV. Round 10-by-10 increments are not 
  possible at 8-bit.

  So: keep the 16-bit DAC if you want clean 10mV/100mV increments. 8-bit forces
  ugly 19.5mV-granular steps. The 16-bit is what makes "increment by 10"
  practical.

  Summary:
  - R7 → 10k (unity)
  - Gain resistors → linear (60k, 30k, 20k, 15k, 12k, 10k…), not ×10
  - Keep 16-bit for round-number voltage increments

● That's the V_CENT injection — R31–R34 (6.2k/62k/620k/6.2M) feeding selectable
  amounts of −8V into the U16 summing amp's inverting node via U23. Through
  Rf=4.7k it adds these positive DC contributions to the output:

  ┌────────┬──────┬──────────┬──────────────┐
  │ Switch │  R   │ from −8V │ Output shift │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼──────────────┤
  │ U23A   │ 6.2k │          │ +6.06V       │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼──────────────┤
  │ U23B   │ 62k  │          │ +0.61V       │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼──────────────┤
  │ U23C   │ 620k │          │ +0.061V      │
  ├────────┼──────┼──────────┼──────────────┤
  │ U23D   │ 6.2M │          │ +0.006V      │
  └────────┴──────┴──────────┴──────────────┘

  (switches combine → crude 4-decade coarse adjust)

  My verdict: not redundant given your current signal path — but you have a 
  choice

  Here's the key insight. Your signal chain currently produces an all-negative
  waveform (your own note: "0 to −9.4V, all negative"). Something must push it
  back up to center it on 0V. That's exactly what V_CENT does — it recenters the
  inherently-offset signal.

  So you have two valid architectures:

  Option A — keep V_CENT (what you have):
  - Signal path stays all-negative
  - V_CENT adds the fixed +DC to recenter it
  - V_OFFSET DAC then does user-adjustable offset on top
  - Benefit: the offset DAC's full range is free for real offset (not wasted on
  recentering). You also get coarse (V_CENT) + fine (V_OFFSET) offset control —
  like a real bench generator.

  Option B — center in stage 1, drop V_CENT:
  - Inject +2.5V reference at U7 (the 20k-to-+5V resistor I mentioned) so the
  signal comes out symmetric (±) already
  - Then V_CENT becomes redundant — offset DAC handles everything
  - Benefit: fewer parts, one less CD4066 + 4 resistors + 4 control bits

  What I'd actually do

  Keep V_CENT. Reasons:
  1. You already built it — no cost to keep
  2. It genuinely offloads recentering from the offset DAC
  3. Coarse + fine offset is a feature, not waste
  4. It cleanly separates "set the zero point" (V_CENT) from "user offset"
  (V_OFFSET DAC)

  One note: the ×10 resistor spacing (6.2k→62k→620k→6.2M) makes it a decade
  adjuster — good for coarse centering, but the steps are big. That's fine for
  its job (set zero once), since the 16-bit offset DAC gives you the fine
  resolution.

  So: overall good, keep it. It's the "coarse zero" knob; the offset DAC is the
  "fine offset" knob.

● You've hit the key realization: you never actually need a −2.5V supply.
  Centering current can come from your existing −8V rail through a resistor. Let
  me write the equations from scratch and solve for the optimal resistor set.

  Function Generator — Output Stage Math

  Step 1 — Stage transfer equations

  Define Vs = signal DAC voltage ∈ [0, 5] V.

  U7 (inverting, R7 = 10k feedback, DAC 10k Thevenin = input R):
  V1 = −(R7 / 10k)·Vs = −Vs          [unity, since R7 = 10k]

  U20 (inverting, gain G = Rf2 / Rg, CD4066-selected):
  V_SIGNAL = −G·V1 = +G·Vs           ∈ [0, +5G]

  U16 (final summing amp, feedback R15 = 4.7k; signal in R20 = 4.7k; offset in
  R17 = 4.7k; centering in R_c from −8V):
  Vout = −(R15/R20)·V_SIGNAL − (R15/R17)·V_OFFSET − R15·(−8 / R_c)

  With R15 = R20 = R17 = 4.7k:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Vout = −G·Vs  −  H·Vo  +  Vc                 │   ← MASTER EQUATION
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

     where   Vc = 8 · (R15 / R_c) = 37 600 / R_c   [volts, R_c in Ω]
             H·Vo = offset DAC contribution (0 … +5H, enters as −)

  Step 2 — What each term does

  ┌───────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │ Term  │           Polarity at output            │        Source        │
  ├───────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ −G·Vs │ the wave, swings 0 → −5G (all negative) │ signal DAC           │
  ├───────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ +Vc   │ positive DC                             │ −8V via R_c (V_CENT) │
  ├───────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ −H·Vo │ negative DC                             │ offset DAC           │
  └───────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

  This is why both V_CENT and the offset DAC exist and neither is redundant: Vc
  pushes the output up (positive), the offset DAC pushes it down (negative).
  Combined DC level D = Vc − H·Vo is bipolar. That was the missing piece — you
  don't need −2.5V, you need the −8V rail (which you have) feeding a positive
  contribution.

  Step 3 — Solve the mode conditions

  The wave −G·Vs runs from 0 (Vs=0) down to −5G (Vs=5). Add DC D:

  output max = D
  output min = D − 5G
  midpoint   = D − 2.5G

  Set each mode:

  Centered  (midpoint = 0):   D − 2.5G = 0   →   D = 2.5G
  Full +    (min = 0):        D − 5G   = 0   →   D = 5G
  Full −    (max = 0):        D        = 0   →   D = 0

  Step 4 — Required centering values (the clean part)

  For centered mode at each gain G = 1…6, D = Vc = 2.5G:

  G:     1     2     3     4     5     6
  Vc:   2.5   5.0   7.5  10.0  12.5  15.0   V

  Every value is a multiple of 2.5V. So design V_CENT as a small DAC with 2.5V 
  resolution, 0–15V range — it lands exactly on every centered setpoint.

  Step 5 — Solve for the resistors

  R_c = 37 600 / Vc        [Ω]

  Make a binary-weighted bank (so combinations give every multiple), using your
  4 switches U23A–D:

  ┌────────────┬───────────┬──────────────┬───────────┐
  │   Switch   │ Target Vc │ R = 37600/Vc │ Use (E96) │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼───────────┤
  │ U23D (LSB) │ 1.25 V    │ 30.1k        │ 30k       │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼───────────┤
  │ U23C       │ 2.5 V     │ 15.0k        │ 15k       │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼───────────┤
  │ U23B       │ 5.0 V     │ 7.52k        │ 7.5k      │
  ├────────────┼───────────┼──────────────┼───────────┤
  │ U23A (MSB) │ 10.0 V    │ 3.76k        │ 3.74k     │
  └────────────┴───────────┴──────────────┴───────────┘

  Switch combinations then give Vc = 0 … 18.75V in 1.25V steps — covers all six
  centered settings and full-positive up to G=3, with the 16-bit offset DAC 
  trimming any residual finely.

  Step 6 — Why your current values are broken

  Present: R31=6.2k, R32=62k, R33=620k, R34=6.2M (decade). Single-switch Vc:

  6.06V,  0.61V,  0.061V,  0.006V

  You can only make 0–0.67V (without R31) or 6.06–6.73V (with R31). There's a
  dead gap from 0.67V to 6.06V — you literally cannot produce 2.5V, the most
  important centering value. Decade spacing is wrong here; binary spacing 
  (30k/15k/7.5k/3.74k) is the fix.

  ---
  Summary of the answer

  1. Unity gain confirmed: R7 = R13 = 10k.
  2. Output equation: Vout = −G·Vs − H·Vo + Vc
  3. Centering ≠ −2.5V supply. Use the −8V rail through R_c; Vc = 37600/R_c.
  4. V_CENT resistors → binary: 30k, 15k, 7.5k, 3.74k (replaces the broken
  decade set). Gives 1.25V steps, 0–18.75V.
  5. Per gain G, set V_CENT = 2.5G for centered; the offset DAC does fine trim +
  the negative side.

  Want me to add this derivation + the new resistor values into
  CIRCUIT_CHANGES.md as the finalized centering section?

❯ also i thought the deafualt offset hsoil it be -8V? ro center how does        
  professional function genratiors work                                         

● Good question — and it exposes an important distinction. −8V is not "the 
  offset." It's a reference rail you tap to make a current. You should never
  default the offset to −8V. Let me explain how real generators do it, then map
  it to your circuit.

  How professional function generators work

  Take a Keysight 33220A / Rigol DG series. Their architecture:

  DAC (bipolar) → reconstruction filter → amplitude amp → [+ offset] summing →
  output amp → attenuator → BNC

  Key design choices:

  1. The DAC is bipolar by design. Mid-scale code = 0V output, not 2.5V. So the
  waveform is already centered on 0 the instant it leaves the DAC. There is no
  separate "centering" step — it's baked into how the code maps to voltage
  (offset-binary: 0x8000 = 0V).
  2. Amplitude and offset are independent knobs. Amplitude sets Vpp (the gain
  stage). Offset adds a DC level in a summing amp — exactly your U16.
  3. Default offset = 0V. Out of the box you get a symmetric wave centered on 0.
  You only dial offset when you want it off-center.
  4. Rail constraint enforced in firmware: |offset| + Vpp/2 ≤ Vmax. Crank offset
  up and the instrument limits your max amplitude — exactly the trade-off you
  intuited earlier.

  The difference in YOUR circuit

  Your signal is inherently all-negative (−G·Vs, swinging 0 → −5G) because your
  DAC is unipolar (0–5V, code 0 = 0V). So unlike a pro gen, you must inject Vc =
  2.5G just to reach the "centered" default. That injection is sourced from −8V
  through R_c — but the value injected is 2.5G, not 8V.

  For centered default at G=1: you want Vc = 2.5V → that's −8V through 15k (one
  switch, U23C). The −8V is just the pressure source; the resistor sets how
  much.

  Two ways to get pro-like behavior

  Option A (what you have — center at U16, post-gain):
  - Default = software turns on the V_CENT switch giving Vc = 2.5G for the
  current gain
  - Default offset (the DAC) = 0
  - Works, but centering value changes every time you change gain

  Option B (pro-style — center the DAC before gain):
  - Subtract the 2.5V midpoint at U7 so the signal is symmetric (±2.5V) before
  amplification
  - Math: inject −8V through R = 32k into U7:
  V1 = −Vs − (10k/32k)·(−8) = −Vs + 2.5 = −(Vs − 2.5)   → ±2.5V centered
  - After gain → ±2.5G, automatically centered at every gain, no per-gain 
  adjustment
  - Default offset = 0 (true pro behavior), offset DAC only used when you want
  offset

  This dissolves your original "−2.5V problem" — you don't need a −2.5V supply,
  you need −8V through 32k. That single resistor makes your DAC behave bipolar,
  just like a real generator.

  My recommendation

  For pro-like behavior, go Option B: one 32k resistor from −8V at U7 centers
  everything, gain-independent, default offset = 0. Then:
  - Amplitude knob = the CD4066 gain bank
  - Offset knob = the 16-bit offset DAC (bipolar, default 0)
  - V_CENT switched bank becomes unnecessary for centering — repurpose it as a
  coarse offset, or drop it

  You keep both 16-bit DACs. The signal DAC + 32k = bipolar source; the offset
  DAC = independent DC offset. That's exactly the professional model.

  Direct answer to your question: default offset should be 0V (centered), not
  −8V. The −8V only supplies the fixed centering current through 32k; the user
  offset sits on top of that and defaults to zero.

  Want me to write Options A vs B side-by-side into CIRCUIT_CHANGES.md with the
  32k centering resistor and the rail-limit formula, so you can pick?
